We have simple poles of the tree-level five-point amplitude in the complex
z_{v}
-plane corresponding to
q_{1}^{2}=0
and
q_{2}^{2}=0
which correspond to factorization channels:

\begin{split}\frac{I_{\mathcal{C}^{(+)}_{q_{1}}}^{\lambda}}{2\pi i}&=\sum_{h}\mathcal{M}_{4,\mathrm{cl}}^{(0)}(p_{2},k^{\lambda},-q_{1}^{h})\mathcal{M}_{3,\mathrm{cl}}^{(0)}(p_{1},q_{1}^{h})\operatornamewithlimits{Res}_{z=\hat{z}_{1}}\frac{-1}{q_{1}^{2}}\,,\\
\frac{I_{\mathcal{C}^{(+)}_{q_{2}}}^{\lambda}}{2\pi i}&=\sum_{h}\mathcal{M}_{4,\mathrm{cl}}^{(0)}(p_{1},k^{\lambda},-q_{2}^{h})\mathcal{M}_{3,\mathrm{cl}}^{(0)}(p_{2},q_{2}^{h})\operatornamewithlimits{Res}_{z=\hat{z}_{2}}\frac{-1}{q_{2}^{2}}\,,\end{split}

(8.20)

where
\hat{z}_{1}
and
\hat{z}_{2}
are the solutions in the UHP of the pole constraints
-q_{i}^{2}=0
.

The pole(s)
\hat{z}_{1}
are given by the solutions of

\displaystyle-q_{1}^{2}=-(z_{1}^{2}+z_{2}^{2}+2\gamma z_{1}z_{2}-z_{v}^{2}-z_{b}^{2})=0\,,

(8.21)

recalling now that in the hatted-kinematics

z_{1}=\dfrac{\gamma}{\gamma^{2}-1}\omega_{2}\,,\qquad z_{2}=-\dfrac{1}{\gamma^{2}-1}\omega_{2}\,,

(8.22)

we find

\displaystyle-q_{1}^{2}=z_{v}^{2}+z_{b}^{2}+\omega_{2,r}^{2}=(z_{v}-\hat{z}_{1})(z_{v}-\hat{z}_{1}^{*})=0\,,

(8.23)

with

\hat{z}_{1}=i\sqrt{z_{b}^{2}+w_{2,r}^{2}}\,.

(8.24)

Meanwhile, the pole(s)
\hat{z}_{2}
are given by the solutions of

\displaystyle-q_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-(k-q_{1})^{2}=-q_{1}^{2}+2k\cdot q_{1}=

(8.25)

\displaystyle=-(z_{1}^{2}+z_{2}^{2}+2\gamma z_{1}z_{2}-z_{v}^{2}-z_{b}^{2})+2z_{1}(k\cdot v_{1})+2z_{2}(k\cdot v_{2})+2z_{v}(k\cdot\tilde{v})+2z_{b}(k\cdot\tilde{b})=0\,,

which, after imposing the constraints in Eq. (
8.22
), becomes

\displaystyle-q_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=z_{v}^{2}+z_{b}^{2}+\omega_{2,r}^{2}+2\gamma\omega_{1,r}\omega_{2,r}-2\omega_{2,r}^{2}+2z_{v}(k\cdot\tilde{v})+2z_{b}(k\cdot\tilde{b})=

\displaystyle=(z_{v}+k\cdot\tilde{v})^{2}+(z_{b}+k\cdot\tilde{b})^{2}-(k\cdot\tilde{v})^{2}-(k\cdot\tilde{b})^{2}-\omega_{2,r}^{2}+2\gamma\omega_{1,r}\omega_{2,r}\,.

(8.26)

Notice now that

-(k\cdot\tilde{v})^{2}=(k\cdot\tilde{b})^{2}+\omega_{1,r}^{2}+\omega_{2,r}^{2}-2\gamma\omega_{1,r}\omega_{2,r}\,,

(8.27)

henceforth Eq. (
8.26
) reduces to

\displaystyle-q_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=(z_{v}+k\cdot\tilde{v})^{2}+(z_{b}+k\cdot\tilde{b})^{2}+\omega_{1,r}^{2}=(z_{v}-\hat{z}_{2})(z_{v}-\hat{z}_{2}^{*})=0\,,

(8.28)

with

\hat{z}_{2}=-\tilde{v}\cdot k+i\sqrt{(z_{b}+\tilde{b}\cdot k)^{2}+w_{1,r}^{2}}\,,

(8.29)

Other than the residues related to these simple poles, we should also take into account (half) the residue at infinity of the five-point amplitude, which we label as
I_{\mathcal{C}^{(+)}_{\infty}}^{\lambda}

16
16

16

It is interesting to notice that the residue at infinity
is completely determined by the leading soft theorem in the classical limit. For a more detailed discussion see App. A of Ref.
[
114
]
.
.

Summarizing, we have the following equality

I_{\text{UHP}}^{\lambda}=I^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{C}^{(+)}_{q_{1}}}+I^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{C}^{(+)}_{q_{2}}}+I^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{C}^{(+)}_{\infty}}\,,

(8.30)

The treatment of the residue at infinity is quite delicate, as it can have spurious pole that cancel only at later stages.
To avoid having to deal with this problem we can use the following trick: we split the
z_{v}
-integration into two equal pieces and deform the integration contour in the UHP and LHP. As a consequence, the contributions from the arcs at infinity cancel each other (
I^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{C}^{(+)}_{\infty}}=I^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{C}^{(-)}_{\infty}}
) and only the finite poles contribute:

\displaystyle\hskip-10.0pt\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\mathrm{d}z_{v}\ \hat{\mathcal{M}}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(I_{\text{UHP}}^{\lambda}-I_{\text{LHP}}^{\lambda}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left(I^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{C}^{(+)}_{q_{1}}}+I^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{C}^{(+)}_{q_{2}}}-I^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{C}^{(-)}_{q_{1}}}-I^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{C}^{(-)}_{q_{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=i\pi\sum_{i=1,2}\left(\underset{z_{v}=z_{i}}{Res}\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)}-\underset{z_{v}=z_{i}^{*}}{Res}\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)}\right)\,.

(8.31)

The direct computation of the
z_{b}
integral is rather involved and evaluates to Bessel functions, as shown in Ref.
[
116
]
and Ref.
[
117
]
; however, since we are interested in the strain at future null infinity we need to compute the waveform in the time domain.
By switching the order of integration, the computation greatly simplifies.

To compute the
\omega
integral, it is useful to extract
all
\omega
dependencies and define the dimensionless variables

w_{i}\to\omega\,\bar{w}_{i}\ ,\,\,\,\bar{w}_{i}=n\cdot v_{i}\,,\quad z_{v}\to\omega\,z_{v}\,,\quad z_{b}\to\omega\,z_{b}\,.

(8.32)

To determine the scaling of
\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}_{5,\rm cl}
with
\omega
, notice that the five-point amplitude has dimension
4-5=-1
, however the gravitational coupling
\kappa
has dimension
-1
and in the perturbative expansion we have
\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(L)}_{5,\rm cl}\sim\kappa^{3+2L}
.
Notice that if we express the Mandelstam variables of
\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}_{5,\rm cl}(q_{1},q_{2},k)
using Eq. (
8.32
), the only dimensionful parameter is
\omega
. Indeed

\begin{gathered}-q_{1}^{2}=\omega^{2}(z_{v}^{2}+z_{b}^{2}+\bar{\omega}^{2}_{2,r})\,,\qquad-q_{2}^{2}=\omega^{2}\left[(z_{v}+n\cdot\tilde{v})^{2}+(z_{b}+n\cdot\tilde{b})^{2}+\bar{\omega}^{2}_{1,r}\right]\,,\\
k^{2}=0\,,\qquad k\cdot q_{1}=\omega\bar{\omega}_{1}\,,\qquad k\cdot q_{2}=\omega\bar{\omega}_{2}\,.\end{gathered}

(8.33)

If we now express
\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}_{5,\rm cl}(q_{1},q_{2},k)
as

\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}_{5,\rm cl}(q_{1},q_{2},k)=\kappa^{3}\omega^{\alpha}f(\bar{\omega}_{i},z_{v},z_{b})\,.

(8.34)

Since
\omega
has dimension
1
, by dimensional analysis we find the
\omega
dependence of this amplitude to be fixed by

[\mathcal{M}_{5,{\rm cl}}^{(0)}]=[\kappa^{3}][\omega^{\alpha}]\quad\Rightarrow\quad-1=-3+\alpha\,.

(8.35)

Since
\omega
has dimension
1
, we finally obtain
\alpha=2
.
Therefore to extract all
\omega
dependencies out of
\mathcal{M}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)}
we rescale it as

17
17

17

If we where to consider higher order contributions the formula would be modified to

\mathcal{M}_{5,\rm cl}^{(L)}\to\omega^{-2+L}\mathcal{M}_{5,\rm cl}^{(L)}
, see Ref.
[
114
]

\mathcal{M}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)}\to\omega^{-2}\mathcal{M}_{5,{\rm cl}}^{(0)}\,.

(8.36)

Using now the fact that, at tree level,

W^{(0)}(b,k^{\pm})=[W^{(0)}(-b,k^{\mp})]^{*}\,,

(8.37)

we are finally able to rewrite Eq. (
8.1
) as:

\displaystyle h^{(0)}(x)=

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{8\pi|\vec{x}\,|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\hat{\mathrm{d}}\omega\ \Big[W^{(0)}(b;k^{-})e^{-i\omega u}\Big]=

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{8\pi|\vec{x}\,|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\hat{\mathrm{d}}\omega\ \left[\int\!\mathrm{d}z_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{b}\,e^{-iz_{b}\sqrt{-b^{2}}}\frac{\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)}}{(4\pi)^{2}m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}e^{-i\omega u}\right]=

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{8\pi|\vec{x}\,|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\hat{\mathrm{d}}\omega\ \left[\int\!\mathrm{d}z_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{b}\,\omega^{2}\,e^{-i\omega\,z_{b}\sqrt{-b^{2}}}\frac{\omega^{-2}\,\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)}}{(4\pi)^{2}m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}e^{-i\omega u}\right]=

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{8\pi|\vec{x}\,|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\hat{\mathrm{d}}\omega\ \left[\int\!\mathrm{d}z_{b}\,e^{-i\omega\,z_{b}\sqrt{-b^{2}}}\frac{\left(I_{\text{UHP}}^{\lambda}-I_{\text{LHP}}^{\lambda}\right)}{2(4\pi)^{2}m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}e^{-i\omega u}\right]=

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{8\pi|\vec{x}\,|}\int\!\mathrm{d}z_{b}\frac{\left(I_{\text{UHP}}^{\lambda}-I_{\text{LHP}}^{\lambda}\right)}{2(4\pi)^{2}m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\hat{\mathrm{d}}\omega\,e^{-i\omega\,(z_{b}\sqrt{-b^{2}}+u)}=

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{8\pi|\vec{x}\,|}\int\!\mathrm{d}z_{b}\frac{\left(I_{\text{UHP}}^{\lambda}-I_{\text{LHP}}^{\lambda}\right)}{2(4\pi)^{2}m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\delta(z_{b}\sqrt{-b^{2}}+u)

(8.38)

Therefore, we have

h^{(0)}(x)=\frac{\kappa}{(4\pi)^{3}|\vec{x}|\sqrt{-b^{2}}}\frac{(I_{\text{UHP}}^{\lambda}-I_{\text{LHP}}^{\lambda})}{4m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\Bigg|_{z_{b}=-u/\sqrt{-b^{2}}}\,,

(8.39)

From this point onwards a direct computation becomes rather involved, therefore I will resort to
Mathematica
to obtain the final result.

8.1
Tree-level waveform for Schwarzschild black holes

We now compute the tree-level scattering waveform for spinless particles by combining Eq. (
8.39
), Eq. (
8.20
) and Eq. (
8.31
) with the three-point and four-point amplitudes previously obtained:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3,\rm cl}^{(0)}(p,k)

\displaystyle=-\kappa\,m^{2}(\varepsilon\cdot v)^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{4,\text{cl}}^{(0)}(p,k_{1},k_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa^{2}m^{2}}{q^{2}}\left(\frac{v\cdot F_{1}\cdot F_{2}\cdot v}{v\cdot k_{1}}\right)^{2}\,,

(8.40)

with
q=(q_{1}-q_{2})/2
.

Substituting in Eq. (
8.39
), we find

\begin{split}&h^{(0)}(x)=\frac{G^{2}m_{1}m_{2}}{|\vec{x}\,|\sqrt{-b^{2}}}\frac{1}{\bar{w}_{1}^{2}\bar{w}_{2}^{2}\sqrt{1+T_{2}^{2}}\left(\gamma+\sqrt{\left(1+T_{1}^{2}\right)\left(1+T_{2}^{2}\right)}+T_{1}T_{2}\right)}\\
&\quad\times\Bigg(\frac{3\bar{w}_{1}+2\gamma\left(2T_{1}T_{2}\bar{w}_{1}-T_{2}^{2}\bar{w}_{2}+\bar{w}_{2}\right)-\left(2\gamma^{2}-1\right)\bar{w}_{1}}{\gamma^{2}-1}f_{1,2}^{2}\\
&\quad-\frac{4\gamma T_{2}\bar{w}_{2}f_{1}+2\left(2\gamma^{2}-1\right)\left[T_{1}\left(1+T_{2}^{2}\right)\bar{w}_{2}f_{1}+T_{2}(T_{1}T_{2}\bar{w}_{1}+\bar{w}_{2})f_{2}\right]}{\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}f_{1,2}\\
&\quad+4\left(1+T_{2}^{2}\right)\bar{w}_{2}f_{1}f_{2}-4\gamma\left(1+T_{2}^{2}\right)\bar{w}_{2}\left(f_{1}^{2}+f_{2}^{2}\right)+2\left(2\gamma^{2}-1\right)\left(1+2T_{2}^{2}\right)\bar{w}_{2}f_{1}f_{2}\Bigg)+\left(1\leftrightarrow 2\right)\ ,\end{split}

(8.41)

where we have defined

\displaystyle T_{i}

\displaystyle\coloneq\frac{\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}\,(u-b_{i}\cdot n)}{\sqrt{-(b_{1}-b_{2})^{2}}\,\bar{w}_{i}}\ ,

(8.42)

\displaystyle S^{2}

\displaystyle\coloneq\frac{T_{1}^{2}-2\gamma T_{1}T_{2}+T_{2}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}-1}-1\ ,

(8.43)

\displaystyle f_{1,2}

\displaystyle=v_{1}\cdot\varepsilon_{k}\,v_{2}\cdot n-v_{1}\cdot n\,v_{2}\cdot\varepsilon_{k}\ ,

(8.44)

\displaystyle f_{i}

\displaystyle=\tilde{b}\cdot\varepsilon_{k}\,v_{i}\cdot n-\tilde{b}\cdot n\,v_{i}\cdot\varepsilon_{k}\ .

(8.45)

8.2
Tree-level waveform for Kerr black holes

Let us now compute tree-level scattering waveform Kerr black holes, that we identify with spinning point particles.
In this case the three-point and the four-point Compton amplitudes are:

\begin{gathered}\mathcal{M}^{(0)}_{3,\rm cl}=-\kappa\,m^{2}\,(\varepsilon\cdot v)^{2}\,\exp\left(\frac{i\,\varepsilon\cdot S\cdot k}{\varepsilon\cdot v}\right)\ ,\\
\mathcal{M}^{(0)}_{4,\rm cl}=\frac{\kappa^{2}m^{2}\omega_{0}^{2}}{8(k_{1}\cdot k_{2})(k_{1}\cdot v)^{2}}\exp\left(\frac{\omega_{1}}{\omega_{0}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(S^{5})\,,\end{gathered}

(8.46)

with

\begin{gathered}\omega_{0}=-2\,v\cdot F_{1}\cdot F_{2}\cdot v\,,\\
i\,\omega_{1}=k_{1}\cdot F_{2}\cdot v\,S\cdot F_{1}-\frac{(k_{1}-k_{2})\cdot v}{2}S\cdot F_{1}\cdot F_{2}+\left(1\leftrightarrow 2\right)\,,\end{gathered}

(8.47)

where
S
is the (mass-normalized) spin tensor of the Kerr black holes and we have defined
A\cdot B\cdot C=A^{\mu\nu}B_{\nu\rho}C^{\rho\sigma}\eta_{\sigma\mu}
and
A\cdot B=A^{\mu\nu}B_{\nu\mu}
.

Note that

\dfrac{\omega_{2}}{\omega_{0}}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\dfrac{\omega_{1}}{\omega_{0}}\right)^{2}\,,

(8.48)

where

\displaystyle\omega_{2}

\displaystyle=(k_{1}\cdot k_{2})\bigg[S\cdot S\left(\frac{F_{1}\cdot F_{2}}{2}-v\cdot F_{1}\cdot F_{2}\cdot v\right)-2\,S\cdot S\cdot F_{1}\cdot F_{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{\omega_{0}}{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})\cdot S\cdot S\cdot(k_{1}+k_{2})\ ,

(8.49)

and
A\cdot B\cdot C\cdot D=A^{\mu\nu}B_{\nu\rho}C^{\rho\sigma}D_{\sigma\mu}
.
Hence, we can write, as anticipated in Sec.
6
, the Compton amplitude up to fifth order in the spin tensor without any spurious pole as

\mathcal{M}^{(0)}_{4,\rm cl}=\kappa^{2}m^{2}\frac{\omega_{0}^{2}+\omega_{0}\omega_{1}+\omega_{0}\omega_{2}+\frac{1}{3}\omega_{1}\omega_{2}+\frac{1}{6}\omega_{2}^{2}}{8(k_{1}\cdot k_{2})(k_{1}\cdot v)^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(S^{5})\,.

(8.50)

We may now be tempted to directly substitute in Eq. (
8.39
) in order to compute the waveform up to the fourth order in spin for both black holes, i.e.
\mathcal{O}(S_{1}^{4},S_{2}^{4})
.
However in this case the discussion is more delicate.
Indeed, after applying the rescaling Eq. (
8.32
), the degree of homogeneity in
\omega
is not the one predicted in Eq. (
8.36
), as the mass-dimension of
S_{i}^{\mu\nu}
is
-1
.
If we write the five-point amplitude in the spin-multipole expansion

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(L)}_{5,{\rm cl}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}=1}^{\infty}\left[S_{1}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\dots S_{1}^{\mu_{s_{1}}\nu_{s_{1}}}\right]\left[S_{2}^{\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}}\dots S_{2}^{\rho_{s_{2}}\sigma_{s_{2}}}\right]\mathcal{M}^{(L),(s_{1},s_{2})}_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1},\dots\mu_{s_{1}}\nu_{s_{1}},\rho_{1}\sigma_{1},\dots\rho_{s_{2}}\sigma_{s_{2}}}\ ,

(8.51)

the scaling will be

\mathcal{M}^{(L),(s_{1},s_{2})}\rightarrow\omega^{-2+L+s_{1}+s_{2}}\mathcal{M}^{(L),(s_{1},s_{2})}\,.

(8.52)

Therefore it is convenient to compute the tree-level five-point spinning waveform order by order in its spin-multipole expansion,
i.e.

h^{(0)}(x)=\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}=0}^{\infty}h^{(s_{1},s_{2})}(x)\,.

(8.53)

Let us now study how the
z_{v}
integral gets modified.

\displaystyle h^{(s_{1},s_{2})}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{8\pi|\vec{x}\,|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\hat{\mathrm{d}}\omega\ \left[\int\!\mathrm{d}z_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{b}\,e^{-iz_{b}\sqrt{-b^{2}}}\frac{\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0),(s_{1},s_{2})}}{(4\pi)^{2}m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}e^{-i\omega u}\right]=

(8.54)

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{8\pi|\vec{x}\,|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\hat{\mathrm{d}}\omega\,\left[\int\!\mathrm{d}z_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{b}\,\omega^{2}\,e^{-i\omega\,z_{b}\sqrt{-b^{2}}}\frac{{\omega^{-2+s_{1}+s_{2}}\,\hat{\mathcal{M}}}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)(s_{1},s_{2})}}{(4\pi)^{2}m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}e^{-i\omega u}\right]=

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{8\pi|\vec{x}\,|}\int\!\mathrm{d}z_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{b}\,\dfrac{\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)(s_{1},s_{2})}}{(4\pi)^{2}m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\hat{\mathrm{d}}\omega\,\omega^{s_{1}+s_{2}}e^{-i\omega(u+z_{b}\sqrt{-b^{2}}})=

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{8\pi|\vec{x}\,|}\int\!\mathrm{d}z_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{b}\,\dfrac{\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)(s_{1},s_{2})}}{(4\pi)^{2}m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\times

\displaystyle\times\left(\dfrac{i}{\sqrt{-b^{2}}}\right)^{s_{1}+s_{2}}\frac{\partial^{(s_{1}+s_{2})}}{\partial z_{b}^{s_{1}+s_{2}}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\hat{\mathrm{d}}\omega\,e^{-i\omega(u+z_{b}\sqrt{-b^{2}}})=

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{8\pi|\vec{x}\,|}\left(\dfrac{i}{\sqrt{-b^{2}}}\right)^{s_{1}+s_{2}}\!\!\int\!\mathrm{d}z_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{b}\,\dfrac{\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)(s_{1},s_{2})}}{(4\pi)^{2}m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\left(\frac{\partial^{(s_{1}+s_{2})}}{\partial z_{b}^{s_{1}+s_{2}}}\delta(u+z_{b}\sqrt{-b^{2}})\right)=

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{2|\vec{x}\,|(4\pi)^{3}m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\dfrac{(-i)^{s_{1}+s_{2}}}{(\sqrt{-b^{2}})^{s_{1}+s_{2}}}\times

\displaystyle\times\int\!\mathrm{d}z_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{b}\,\delta(u+z_{b}\sqrt{-b^{2}})\left(\frac{\partial^{(s_{1}+s_{2})}}{\partial z_{b}^{s_{1}+s_{2}}}\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)(s_{1},s_{2})}\right)=

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{2|\vec{x}\,|(4\pi)^{3}m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\dfrac{(-i)^{s_{1}+s_{2}}}{(\sqrt{-b^{2}})^{s_{1}+s_{2}+1}}\frac{\partial^{(s_{1}+s_{2})}}{\partial z_{b}^{s_{1}+s_{2}}}\int\!\mathrm{d}z_{v}\,\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{5,\rm cl}^{(0)(s_{1},s_{2})}\Bigg|_{z_{b}=-u/\sqrt{-b^{2}}}

Consequently, the spinning tree-level waveform can be computed order by order, isolating the
s_{1}^{\rm th}
and
s_{2}^{\rm th}
multipoles in the factorized amplitudes, as

\hskip-2.0pth^{(s_{1},s_{2})}(x)=\frac{\kappa}{(4\pi)^{3}\,|\vec{x}|\,(\sqrt{-b^{2}})^{s_{1}+s_{2}+1}}\frac{(-i)^{s_{1}+s_{2}}}{4m_{1}m_{2}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\frac{\partial^{(s_{1}+s_{2})}}{\partial z_{b}^{s_{1}+s_{2}}}(I_{\text{UHP}}^{(s_{1},s_{2})}-I_{\text{LHP}}^{(s_{1},s_{2})})\Bigg|_{z_{b}=-u/\sqrt{-b^{2}}}\,.

(8.55)

In conclusion, we have

h^{(0)}(x)=\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}=0}^{4}\dfrac{G^{2}m_{1}m_{2}}{|\vec{x}|(\sqrt{-b^{2}})^{s_{1}+s_{2}+1}}\mathfrak{h}_{s_{1},s_{2}}(x)+\mathcal{O}(S_{1}^{5},S_{2}^{5})\,,

(8.56)

where the
\mathfrak{h}_{s_{1},s_{2}}
’s are provided in the
ancillary files
obtained in Ref.
[
114
]
.

9
Conclusions

In this thesis, I have studied the classical scattering problem of two Kerr black holes in general relativity using novel quantum field theory techniques within the Post-Minkowskian expansion, extending the subleading soft theorem to account for spinning particles.

I began by describing the spinor-helicity formalism, which served as a foundational tool for computing basic amplitudes. I then introduced a general method for obtaining higher-point amplitudes, utilizing the BCFW recursion relations to compute them in a general context. This was later refined to the soft recursion relations, which I applied to compute
(n+1)
-point amplitudes involving one additional external graviton, starting from the corresponding
n
-point amplitudes.

With these tools in place, I computed the gravitational Compton amplitude for both spinless and spinning black holes, and the elastic 2-to-2 black hole scattering amplitudes. I also derived the five-point amplitude, which represents the first original result in this thesis. While the five-point amplitude exists in the literature, to my knowledge, no prior work has computed it using soft recursion on the four-point amplitude.

In parallel, I emphasized the necessity of a classical formulation for scattering amplitudes and introduced the KMOC formalism, detailing its complexities and offering a general method for computing observables. In this context, I re-evaluated all quantum amplitudes to derive their classical counterparts, presenting a straightforward method for calculating the classical (infinite-spin) limit.

Additionally, I introduced a classical version of the soft recursion relations to derive higher-point classical amplitudes from lower-point ones in a purely classical setting—an approach that, to my knowledge, is presented here for the first time.

Finally, I applied the KMOC formalism to compute the leading-order waveform for black hole scattering with radiation emission, covering both the spinning and spinless cases.

There are several compelling future directions that can be explored:

•

Investigating the structure of spurious poles starting from the
\mathcal{O}(S^{4})
term in the Compton amplitude for spinning particles could provide new insights into the dynamics of spinning black holes.

•

A deeper understanding of the higher orders in the five-point amplitude, especially in connection with effects like the Spin Memory Effect, could pave the way for more accurate calculations of spin-related phenomena in classical black hole scattering.

•

Further exploration of the relationship between soft factors and asymptotic symmetries is another promising avenue.

•

It would also be valuable to investigate the double copy structure of soft theorems for massive particles.

•

Finally, it wold be interesting to extend these results to higher-loop or PM orders, including radiative corrections and finite-size effects.

10
